I felt like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing—my paintings and my client papers. I didn’t think of it like special powers, but I did feel like I had an important role that other people weren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt down, depressed, or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… when I’m not all wound up, I can get really stuck. I’ll sit there for hours with my coffee going cold, not answering the phone, not going to my walking club, and I don’t even care to pick up my paintbrush. I just kind of shut down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where you were more talkative or took on a lot of projects all at once?\n\nPatient: Yes. I get this kind of energy where I can’t sit still. I’ll be up cleaning the whole kitchen, then start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and start rewriting care schedules all in the same night. It’s like I keep jumping from one thing to the next and I can’t make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How long do these energized periods usually last? Do they go on for days at a time?\n\nPatient: They do go on for days, yes. It’s not just one night. These charged-up stretches, where I’m barely sleeping and chasing all these plans, have been coming and going for… I’d say about 11 months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about the two-weeks-straight part. I know I’ve had days where I feel really low and don’t want to do much, but I haven’t kept track well enough to say it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: When I’m not all wound up, I can sit for hours with my coffee getting cold, not answering calls, not going to the walking club, and not even caring to pick up my paintbrush.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How long do these energized periods usually last? Do they go on for days at a time?\n\nPatient: They do go on for days, yes. It’s not just one night. These charged-up stretches, where I’m barely sleeping and chasing all these plans, have been coming and going for… I’d say about 11 months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about the two-weeks-straight part. I know I’ve had days where I feel really low and don’t want to do much, but I haven’t kept track well enough to say it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve felt down at times, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight. I haven’t really kept track of it like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a period where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. They say my name, and they tell me my work is being judged, even when the house is quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things or feel physical sensations that others don’t, like seeing people or feeling something touching you when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see people or feel things touching me, no. It’s more the voices. They sound clear, like someone’s right there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like there are thoughts or ideas that are put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being controlled or shared with others in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head exactly. It’s more the voices. They sound clear, like someone’s right there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like there are thoughts or ideas that are put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being controlled or shared with others in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head exactly. I felt like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing—my paintings and my client papers. Other people told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the media, like TV shows, music, or books, have special messages meant just for you, or that you have a special role or importance that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t know so much about TV or music sending me messages. But I did feel like I had this special purpose with my art. I really believed my paintings were about to get discovered any day, and that I was supposed to make this whole care-and-art program, like something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your art or the feeling of being watched, have you believed that you had a special role or importance that others didn’t recognize, like being chosen for a unique mission or having special powers?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was tied to the art, yes. They say my name, and they tell me my work is being judged, even when the house is quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things or feel physical sensations that others don’t, like seeing people or feeling something touching you when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see people or feel things touching me, no. It’s more the voices. They sound clear, like someone’s right there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like there are thoughts or ideas that are put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being controlled or shared with others in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head exactly. But I did get this strong idea that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and my client papers, like they were trying to steal an opportunity that was supposed to be mine. People told me that didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like certain people or organizations are specifically targeting you, or that events happening around you have a special meaning that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I felt like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing—my paintings and my client papers. Like they were trying to take some chance that was meant for me. Other people told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the media, like TV shows, music, or books, have special messages meant just for you, or that you have a special role or importance that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t know so much about TV or music sending me messages. But I did feel like I had this special purpose with my art. I really believed my paintings were about to get discovered any day, and that I was supposed to make this whole care-and-art program, like something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your art or the feeling of being watched, have you believed that you had a special role or importance that others didn’t recognize, like being chosen for a unique mission or having special powers?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was tied to the art, yes. I really believed my paintings were going to get discovered any day, like it was just about to happen. And I thought I was supposed to build this whole care-and-art program, something doctors and agencies would come to me to learn about. I didn’t think of it like special powers, but I did feel like I had an important role that other people weren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt down, depressed, or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… when I’m not all wound up, I can get really stuck. I know I’ve had days where I feel really low and don’t want to do much, but I haven’t kept track well enough to say it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF25.0, F25.1, F25.2, F25.8, F25.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing voices. It sounds real clear, like somebody is standing right there in my little studio. They say my name, and they tell me my work is being judged, even when the house is quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see things or feel physical sensations that others don’t, like seeing people or feeling something touching you when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see people or feel things touching me, no. It’s more the voices. They sound clear, like someone’s right there in my studio, saying my name and talking about my work being judged, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like there are thoughts or ideas that are put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being controlled or shared with others in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head exactly. But I did get this strong idea that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and my client papers, like they were trying to steal an opportunity that was supposed to be mine. People told me that didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew they were doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like certain people or organizations are specifically targeting you, or that events happening around you have a special meaning that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Yes. I felt like that other home-care agency, and some people at the library, were watching what I was doing—my paintings and my client papers. Like they were trying to take some chance that was meant for me. Other people told me it didn’t make sense, but I still felt like I knew it was happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things in the media, like TV shows, music, or books, have special messages meant just for you, or that you have a special role or importance that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t know so much about TV or music sending me messages. But I did feel like I had this special purpose with my art. I really believed my paintings were about to get discovered any day, and that I was supposed to make this whole care-and-art program, like something doctors and agencies would come learn from me about.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your art or the feeling of being watched, have you believed that you had a special role or importance that others didn’t recognize, like being chosen for a unique mission or having special powers?\n\nPatient: Mostly it was tied to the art, yes. I really believed my paintings were going to get discovered any day, like it was just about to happen. And I thought I was supposed to build this whole care-and-art program, something doctors and agencies would come to me to learn about. I didn’t think of it like special powers, but I did feel like I had an important role that other people weren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt down, depressed, or hopeless for most of the day, nearly every day, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… when I’m not all wound up, I can get really stuck. I’ll sit there for hours with my coffee going cold, not answering the phone, not going to my walking club, and I don’t even care to pick up my paintbrush. I just kind of shut down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where you were more talkative or took on a lot of projects all at once?\n\nPatient: Yes. I get this kind of energy where I can’t sit still. I’ll be up cleaning the whole kitchen, then start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and start rewriting care schedules all in the same night. It’s like I keep jumping from one thing to the next and I can’t make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How long do these energized periods usually last? Do they go on for days at a time?\n\nPatient: They do go on for days, yes. It’s not just one night. These charged-up stretches, where I’m barely sleeping and chasing all these plans, have been coming and going for… I’d say about 11 months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about the two-weeks-straight part. I know I’ve had days where I feel really low and don’t want to do much, but I haven’t kept track well enough to say it was nearly every day for two full weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve felt down at times, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight. I haven’t really kept track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt down, hopeless, or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I still can’t really say that for sure. I’ve had times where I felt down or didn’t feel like doing anything, but I don’t know if it was most of the day nearly every day for two weeks. I haven’t kept track that closely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and recall if there was a time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I just can’t really recall it that clearly. I know I’ve had stretches where I felt low, but I can’t say for sure it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: I can’t think of a specific time like that, no. I’ve had low days and times I felt hopeless, but I can’t honestly say it was most of the day, every day, for two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in the past when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks or more in a row?\n\nPatient: No, I still can’t think of a time like that for sure. I’ve felt down before, but I don’t remember it being most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of any time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I keep trying to think of a time like that, but I can’t pin one down. I’ve had bad days, sure, but I can’t say it was most of the day every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of any time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I really can’t say that I have. I know I’ve had times I felt down, but looking back, I don’t remember it being most of the day, nearly every day, for two full weeks.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of any time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a few times, but no… I still can’t really think of a stretch like that. I’ve been sad or low at times, but not where I can say it was most of the day, nearly every day, for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you think of any time when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times I felt bad, but I can’t remember it lasting like that all day, almost every day, for two weeks straight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
